#### Existing Expected Points (in database)
- Technological nature refers to computer-generated images that simulate real natural scenes.
- The study investigated whether virtual nature views provide the same health benefits as viewing real nature.
- Participants were tested under stress, comparing the recovery rates in rooms with real versus virtual views.
- The findings demonstrated that real natural views significantly accelerate the body's recovery from stress compared to virtual views.

#### Gemma 4 Extracted Main Points

- The study questioned if virtual nature views offer the same health benefits as real natural views.
- Participants were tested under stress, comparing heart rate recovery in rooms with real versus virtual views.
- The results showed that real natural views significantly accelerate recovery from stress compared to virtual views.

### Question 2: #2 Criminal Trials

- **Status:** 🟢 PASS
- **Factual Accuracy:** All existing points are factually accurate and directly supported by the transcript.
- **Coverage:** The existing points cover the entire scope of the lecture, moving logically from the importance of memory to its inherent flaws and the specific mechanism (new information/cross-examination) that causes distortion.

#### Existing Expected Points (in database)
- Memory is a critical and foundational element in criminal trials, relied upon by both prosecution and defense.
- It is well-established that human memory is inherently unreliable.
- The unreliability is particularly problematic when new facts or information are introduced during cross-examination.
- Research demonstrates that introducing new details can significantly skew or distort a witness's original recollection of an event.

#### Gemma 4 Extracted Main Points
- Memory is a fundamental and critical component in criminal trials, utilized by both the prosecution and defense.
- It is widely recognized that human memory is inherently unreliable.
- This unreliability is exacerbated when new, previously unknown facts are introduced during cross-examination.
- Research confirms that introducing new information can significantly distort or skew a witness's original memory of an event.


#### Notes
The existing expected main points are excellent. They capture the core academic concepts (criticality, unreliability, mechanism of distortion, and evidence) in a structured and summary-friendly manner, making them ideal for a PTE SST task.

### Question 17: #17 Philosopher

- **Status:** 🟢 PASS
- **Factual Accuracy:** All existing points are factually accurate and directly supported by the transcript.
- **Coverage:** The existing points cover the core philosophical concepts (Nagel, other minds, subjectivity, prediction vs. inner experience) and the key examples (color red) necessary for a comprehensive summary. They capture the main argumentative flow.
- **Conciseness:** The points are generally concise and distinct. Point 4, while useful, slightly overlaps with Point 2, but the combination provides a strong, multi-faceted summary structure.

#### Existing Expected Points (in database)
- The philosopher Thomas Nagel used the bat thought experiment to question the limits of understanding other minds.
- While we can observe and predict human behavior accurately, we cannot know the inner thoughts or subjective experiences of others.
- Human subjective experiences, such as perceiving colors, are highly individual and can vary greatly beyond cultural differences.
- The difficulty in knowing another person's inner experience is highlighted by the contrast between prediction based on observation and true subjective knowledge.

#### Gemma 4 Extracted Main Points
- Thomas Nagel's bat thought experiment illustrates the difficulty of understanding non-physical subjective experiences.
- While we can observe and predict human behavior based on external signs, we cannot know the inner thoughts or subjective feelings of another person.
- Even seemingly universal experiences, like perceiving the color red, are highly subjective and can vary greatly between individuals, even beyond cultural influences.
- The core limitation is the gap between observable behavior/prediction and true subjective knowledge (qualia).


#### Notes
The existing points are excellent and require minimal revision. They successfully distill the complex philosophical argument into manageable, distinct summary points.
